# Domain decision register

Every product or domain decision by Daniel (CEO) gets one entry here, **the same day** it is made
([rite C5](../process/STRUCTURE.md#c5-decision-to-code)). An entry is the link between the conversation and the code:
it names the invariant in [CONTRACT.md](CONTRACT.md) it creates or changes, and the test that guarantees it.
`test pending` means no automated proof exists yet; the planned PR that adds it is named. A decision is not "done"
until its test exists. The structure owner reviews this file in every fit review.

Format: `D-NNN`, date (America/Sao_Paulo), source, rule, invariant, proof, status.

| D-037 | 2026-10-23 (Daniel, chat) | **A public page sends only the fields it renders, and no Data Cache entry approaches 2 MB.** The universities index, Home, authors and a university page pass the client a view model. A university card is slug, name, fullName, place, cover, courseCount and hero. `readUncached` loads the selected projections with one `readProjections` call, cached by release number and manifest sha, each entry at most 1.5 MB. `getCourseMedia` reads the named owners and the columns a page renders, with the same cap. | D-064 | 2026-10-26 13:47–13:53 (Daniel, chat; recorded by the roadmap implementer under A Torre's delegation, D-054) | **Modular process, no bottleneck.** Two house values drive it: *agility*, so that work gets done "in a magic pass", and *robustness*, so that tomorrow is much easier than today. Rules (a)–(e) serve both. Context (five whys, Daniel 13:50): step 6 fronts queued behind each other; because they all edited the same files (the exception list, STATUS, the common values); because a value used by several areas had no common home and counts were stored in one shared file; because each module's work was planned without looking at what the others touched; because the step plans had no throughput criterion. The root is the last one: planning had no throughput criterion. Rules: **(a) no shared control file.** Counts and registries are kept per module; a total is always computed, never stored; STATUS is written only by integration. **(b) isolated test resources by default.** Each worktree has its own database and port; one gate never wipes another's. **(c) a value used by 2+ modules is born in the common catalog** (`apps/web/src/kernel/`, D-063); the gate refuses a new literal present in 2+ areas that is not there. What is not yet covered is a registered, documented list, not a silent baseline. **(d) parallelism simulation before a stage opens.** A script shows the files touched by N fronts; the common files are resolved first. **(e) throughput criterion.** Every new roadmap step is born with a deadline target, a target number of parallel fronts, and the simulation of (d) run before it starts; the step plan records all three. **(f) gate time ceiling (Daniel, chat, 2026-10-26 14:22):** every `scripts/gate-dev` run has a wall-clock ceiling of 180 s (5 minutes is inadmissible); a run above it prints a clear warning with each stage's time, and the overrun is an item to fix before the next front goes on. **Amendment (2026-10-26 14:15, A Torre, structural, step 6 parallel control):** a catalog file the literal manifest declares (`scripts/lib/literal-catalogs/<area>.json`) is data, not code, and counts toward neither a context's size budget (L3.context-size, D-060) nor the D-008 PR size (B5); Speed must be sustainable: every PR also improves the engine and there are no shortcuts. Today's work must not hinder tomorrow's: avoidable rework is forbidden, rewriting the architecture is not (P-29; Daniel 16:18: "I did not say the architecture cannot be rewritten"). 1,000 PRs/day is only the start; 10k, 100k and 1M per day are the aspiration (P-27). Rewriting the architecture is allowed and welcome (P-28); the old premise "scale without a rewrite" is refuted (P-00). Production stays frozen and reversible (D-054 unchanged); security rules are unchanged; no quality threshold is loosened. **(1) Event-driven flow, never a clock cadence.** Every step is triggered by an event; all state lives in the forge (PRs, refs, commit statuses). Transport v0: the producer writes the event to the forge; when the consumer is a locked role (train, battery), the producer tries to take the lock and runs the role until its queue is empty. The event contract is `docs/process/EVENTS.md` (PR1 of the throughput plan). **(2) Forge-agnostic.** GitHub stays the source of truth for now (merges count there), because Claude Code cloud sessions only work with GitHub. Every tool speaks the event contract through one port, `scripts/lib/forge_events.py` (M2), with a GitHub adapter today and Cursor Origin as the destination adapter; GitHub must not become a design dependency. The *lane*: one long session that exclusively owns a set of units; one lane per unit at a time; the PRs of a unit are serial; lanes run in parallel. Until the unit manifests exist (U0), a lane's ownership is provisional and equals the old front paths. A merge train is the only merger once it exists; until then the current rule holds: gate-dev green on the exact head, merged with `--match-head-commit` as a merge commit. **(5) Gate in three tiers, each tied to an event.** Tier 1 (PR): on a ready commit, the affected checks (the unit battery once units exist), target ≤ 10 s. Tier 2 (batch): on `pr.ready`, run by the train on the combined head, the full dev plan; it blocks the merge. Tier 3 (integration): on `integration.advanced`, coalescing to the newest tip, the full battery; it blocks the release; WebKit runs only at `release.ready`. Tiers 2 and 3 never use cached test results. **(6) Progressive waves** from general to specific; fine tuning only when an event asks for it (`gate.slow`, `train.queue.growing`, `test.flaked`, `session.cold`). **Daniel's vetoes (plan v3 §9.3):** V0 premises with a blast radius (an unconfirmed high-radius premise blocks only the work that depends on it; V6 a tier-1 result cache; V7 Neon gate-db and WebKit only at release, flakes quarantined with an owner; V8 the rewrite is step 7 of D-054 brought forward and merged with the rest of step 6, registered as D-067 in U0. **Forthcoming decisions:** D-066, the premise register (PR P1); A record names `id` (`P-NN`), `slug`, `statement`, `kind`, `scope`, `source` (who, where, literal quote, when), `status`, `radius`, `blast` (reasons, what changes if it is wrong), `validate`, `answer` and `dependents`. *Statuses:* `inferred` and `to-validate` (open), `confirmed` (an answer with a literal quote or a link to the message; a paraphrase is never `confirmed`, it stays `inferred`), `validated` (a measurement with its evidence), `refuted` and `superseded` (refused). Daniel answers high-radius questions; A Torre answers within its delegation (`answer.by: A Torre`). *Radius:* `low`, `medium`, `high`; a reason of kind decision, veto, gate, migration, production, security or move makes it high, three or more units medium; the computed radius may be raised by hand, never lowered. *Dependents* are computed from the `Premises:` lines, written only by the tool and sealed with a digest, so a hand edit fails; the live graph is `scripts/premises blast`, and no aggregate index is committed (P-10: `scripts/premises list` writes `PREMISES.md` under the ignored `.gate/premises/`, with the next question batch). *The `Premises:` line:* every plan and PR body carries `Premises: P-NN, …` (or `none`) next to the B5 trailers; `scripts/premises find` runs before `propose`. `scripts/premises check` and `structure-check` (the `structure check` stage, with `--pr` or `--pr-body`) refuse an unknown id, a refuted or superseded premise, and an unsettled high-radius premise; an unsettled medium premise warns and a low one is only counted. A PR that adds or changes a premise's record is the answer itself and cites it whatever its status. structure-check also validates every record (schema, unique ids and slugs, file name, radius not below the computed one, the dependents seal) and refuses a committed `PREMISES.md`. *v0 → v1:* in v0 a PR body without a `Premises:` line is a warning, so open PRs (e.g. #489) are not broken; v1 flips the registered flag `premises.missing_line_fails` (config/registry/premises.json) to true in one PR citing this decision, after the open PRs carry the line, and the train (M3) allocates ids at merge. | D-068 | 2026-10-26 17:51 (Daniel, CEO, chat; recorded by motor-5 under A Torre's delegation, D-054; plan v3 PR C0) | **No engine parameter is a literal: every setting lives in the configuration registry and is read and changed through its API.** Daniel (pt-BR, literal): "Tudo que for parâmetro, tudo que for configuração, cara, não pode ser literal. Isso é uma configuração bom que seja uma API, de forma que a gente não precisa alterar o código." (1) **Registry.** Each owner declares its parameters in one file `config/registry/<owner>.json`, a JSON Schema 2020-12 object that satisfies `contracts/config/registry.v1.json`. A parameter is named `<owner>.<snake_case_key>` for what it controls (one value, one name) and declares its type, default, valid range or enum, unit (`x-unit`), description and readers (`x-readers`); a feature flag is a boolean with `x-flag`, user-facing text a locale-keyed object with `x-text`. Durations, timeouts, retry counts, seeds, thresholds, model choices, flags, SLOs and budgets are all entries. (2) **Reading.** Python calls `config.get("<name>")` from `scripts/lib/config.py`; shell and `.mjs` run `python3 scripts/lib/config.py get <name>`; never an inline fallback. (3) **Changing without code.** The effective value is the registry default overridden by the store, the append-only log of `config.changed` events on the `forge_events` port (D-065 (1) and (2); adapter `config.store_adapter`); `scripts/config set` validates the value against its schema and records who, when and why; every change is reversible (`scripts/config history`, `scripts/config revert <change>`); a registry default changes by a reviewed commit, and a parameter is removed only after a version that marks it `deprecated` (expand→contract). (4) **Secrets never enter the registry**: a key or value that looks like a secret is refused; only the secret's name may appear. (5) **One schema language**: JSON Schema 2020-12 for config, events, unit contracts and intents, versioned in the file name (`<name>.v<N>.json`); an existing version changes only compatibly (add optional fields, widen), a breaking shape is a new version, and the old one is deleted only when nothing references it; Python types are generated from the schemas, never hand-written. (6) **Gate.** `scripts/structure-check` refuses a literal of a registered parameter in the engine and tooling paths the registry names, an inline fallback, a registry file that does not validate, a secret, an incompatible contract change and generated types that drift; a literal that predates the registry is listed on its parameter (`x-literal-sites`), and the list only shrinks against the base. Extends D-063 (one documented catalog key per value in `apps/web`) to the engine and tooling; the D-065 defaults (batch size B, tier-1 target, `gate.slow`, release cadence) become registry entries, and the event that changes them is `config.changed`. | D-069 | 2026-10-26 17:55 (Daniel, CEO, chat; decided by A Torre; recorded by motor-5 under A Torre's delegation, D-054) | **Engineering standard for every change, aimed at 1k, then 10k, eventually 1M PRs per day.** Daniel (pt-BR, literal): "se a gente tivesse trabalhando na SpaceX, numa empresa top de elite mundial, o que que eles iam fazer pra de fato deixar tudo bonitinho, tudo certinho, tudo de forma profissional? Com a nossa meta em mente, né, de ter mil PRs, dez mil PRs [...] Eu quero isso, quero esse padrão pra gente." (1) One source of truth per fact; docs, aggregates, types and CODEOWNERS are generated from the manifests, the config registry and the event catalog, and the gate fails on drift. (2) Contracts are schema-first in one schema language (JSON Schema 2020-12, D-068), versioned, with generated types; they change by expand→contract and the gate checks compatibility. (3) Configuration as code with an API (D-068, P-41): typed, validated, documented, audited (who, when, why), reversible; secrets never in the registry. (4) The event log is append-only; events are versioned and idempotent; every engine state is rebuildable by replay. (5) Tests are hermetic and deterministic: no network, isolated resources, seeded randomness, clock and timeouts from config; a flaky test goes to quarantine with an owner; each unit has a test budget. (6) Observability from day one: every engine step emits `engine.step.measured` into the event log; SLOs live in config; the dashboard is derived. (7) Ports and adapters for every vendor (forge, agent provider, model ladder, CI runner, storage). (8) Each unit has a generated owner; a change outside the unit needs its owner. (9) Fix the class, not the instance: every incident ends with a gate check that prevents it, recorded in a blameless postmortem (`docs/process/postmortems/`). (10) Decisions in ADRs; premises carry their blast radius; Daniel confirms only with a literal quote. (11) Small, trunk-based, reversible changes; incomplete work behind a flag, and flags are config. (12) Reproducible builds: toolchain versions pinned in config, lockfiles verified, the same command locally and in the cloud. (13) Least privilege: each agent gets only the keys it needs; the gate runs secret scanning, a dependency allowlist and lockfile integrity. Tudo que deixa a gente separado, a gente tem que ter um procedimento padrão pra resolver isso. Porque tempo é mais importante que dinheiro." (02:11); "antes de ter isso tudo bem refinado, acho que o primeiro ponto é colocar uma etapa de avaliação de, cara, que que eu vou fazer? Ocorreu um bloqueio, que que eu vou, que, que bloqueio é esse e o que eu vou fazer com esse bloqueio" (02:14); and he refused absolutes in the same turn: "mas essas regras de nunca ou nada, não sei se eu concordo não." So the step is a procedure, not an absolute rule. Any blocking event (a gate stage red, a base-red, a flaky test, an environment failure, a missing permission or credential, a threshold that stops a build the PR did not change) is evaluated before any action, and the evaluation answers four questions in writing: what exactly is blocked; what it costs in queue terms (which PRs and units wait), never in clock terms (P-32); which of the three options applies; and what was decided, and why. The three options, none of them forbidden, are judged case by case: (a) fix the cause now, (b) unblock now with the cause fix scheduled in a tracked record that names the cause, an owner and a deadline, (c) stop everything and fix. The record is what separates an unblock from pushing the problem under the rug: an unblock is legitimate exactly when that record exists, and an unblock with no record is what the step refuses. The step extends the known-defect route (KD1, D-069 item 15), whose scope today is the test stages of `defects.stages`; a build or budget failure on the base has no such route, which is why 173 bytes of web bundle stopped PR #742 and every unit behind it (first occurrence record: [2026-10-28-web-bundle-blocks-the-queue](../process/incidents/2026-10-28-web-bundle-blocks-the-queue.md), whose decision raises `budgets.web_bundle_bytes_max` to 696320 with the cause fixed by TX13, PR #756, and the ceiling reverted when #756 merges). A blocking event that stops everything is one PR carrying its own audit, plan, fix, gate and retro, never a mini program of many PRs (Daniel, 2026-10-27 23:01). Where a class of block keeps recurring, the occurrence records are the evidence for extending KD1 to it. | D-076 | 2026-10-29 15:51 BRT (Daniel, CEO, via A Torre; pt-BR, literal; D-074 is #768's, D-075 is #802's) | **The gate's verdict is fast: content-addressed reuse, incremental work and parallel runs (GATE-FAST); amends D-065 (5), P-43 and the `--cold` meaning of D-075.** Daniel (pt-BR, literal): "Então implementa isso, né, chefia." — approving, for the merge train toward 1,000 PRs/day, a gate incremental by touched units, a cache keyed by content instead of turning every cache off for a cold verdict, and parallelism (tests in parallel, several verdicts at once), to take a cold verdict from ~12 min to ~1-2 min. (a) **Content-addressed reuse (P-56, partially refutes P-43).** A script test's result, or a deterministic stage's, is a function of its key: the content of every file it reads (its declared inputs), the attested `toolchain_id` (GX-28) and the judging tooling's `gate_version`. A recorded PASS under exactly that key is the same result whichever run or tier wrote it, so tiers 1 and 2 reuse it (`gate-tier.content_cacheable_tiers`, default `[1, 2]`; `gate.content_cache_enabled`, default on, is the one-key rollback to D-065 (5)). Tier 3 (the battery, `gate-noite`) never reuses: it is the periodic full net. (b) **`--cold` means "no result of another tree", not "no cache".** A cold verdict still never reads a recorded verdict and never carries a stage over from another head (carry-over reuses a result by head, not by content); it reuses only content-keyed results. `train_admit.admissible` accepts the hits of a verdict whose `gate_version` is the tip's (that tooling keys every cached result by content), with the train's `gate.content_cache_enabled` on; a carried stage still refuses it. (c) **Incremental.** The work a diff owes is the tests whose declared inputs it touches, through the existing per-test covers, the unit graph (`units+dependents`) and the stage input declarations; a path that cannot be attributed still runs everything (fail closed). (d) **Parallel.** Tests inside a gate run on workers sized to the vCPUs; verdicts of distinct PRs run at once, each in its own worktree and slot (`gate.slots_per_vcpu` 0.5: two on 4 vCPUs), and the train's resync runs the verdicts of the next behind-the-tip PRs beside the oldest one's (`train.reverdict_parallel`, 0 = the slots), while the train alone serializes admission and merge (D-065 V3). The flow becomes INTAKE → RESEARCH → AUDIT → PLAN → execution → RETRO. (1) INTAKE, `intake.md`, the adapter from an external request to our understanding: `Pedido` (literal, not interpreted), `Intenção` (the real why), `Contexto` (state, deadlines, what came before, dependencies), `Regras` (D-NNN, premises, Padrão rules), `Problema real` (one sentence + the metric of resolved), `Lacunas` (what is unknown and on whom it depends; if only the requester knows, ask before spending effort). (2) RESEARCH, `research.md`, looks outward from the intake's real problem: `Quem já resolveu` (3–5 real references with link and what each solves), `Estado da arte` (the standard and the most advanced solution, what each requires), `Encaixe em nós` (copy / adapt / does not fit, with the reason), `Escada de ambição` (the minimum useful step now → the path to the state of the art, its first rung the plan's cheapest useful effort, D-069 item 4); about one page and ~15 minutes, one line when the reference is obvious. (3) **Decoupling** (Daniel's explicit rule): each step delivers its complete, independent product and never dictates what the next one does; the research raises no questions for the audit, the audit builds its own; the next step only reads the previous product. Installed as Padrão v6 (`docs/padrao/v6/intake-and-research.md`, templates `docs/process/templates/`). **Not retroactive:** the `structure check` stage refuses a `docs/units/*` or `docs/programs/*` folder whose oldest commit is at or after `intake.since` (`config/registry/intake.json`, the approval moment) without both files, or with a section missing or empty (`scripts/lib/unit_intake.py`); older folders are not judged. Gate change under D-025. ## Resolved questions

Q1–Q6, open in the proposed design, were answered by Daniel on 2026-10-22 and are now D-014 … D-019.

| Id | Question | Decision | Implemented in |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Does a course ever borrow its institution's cover? | D-014: never | S05b |
| Q2 | Area cover sets, Figma first? | D-015: yes, Education first | S05a, S05b (+ Figma) |
| Q3 | University row 1:1 with one institution? | D-016: yes | S06 |
| Q4 | Must a published course have an area? | D-017: yes; "More courses" removed | S06 |
| Q5 | YouTube Data API vs. oEmbed? | D-018: Data API | S13 |
| Q6 | Who approves generated texts? | D-019: separate automatic reviewer | S14 |

| D-291 | 02/11 16:19 BRT | **Every Tower VM job has one budget, `service.job_budget_s = 10 s`, from claim to its definite answer and release of its resources.** Daniel, 02/11 15:58 BRT: "A VM tem 10 segundos para realizar tudo - seja dar positivo e seguir, seja dar negativo e estar liberada para o próximo". 1. The runner starts accounting before the claim request, refreshes before claiming, and claims one job at a time. An outer alarm covers head checks, Python work and result reporting. The child gets an execution deadline in `TOWER_JOB_DEADLINE` and its owner in `TOWER_JOB_OWNER`; the final two seconds remain inside the budget for cleanup and reporting. TERM precedes the execution deadline by one second, KILL enforces it, and descendants are killed even after their leader exits. Every outcome carries `reason` and `phases`; an exhausted budget is `over_budget`. 2. The train lease ends at the child's deadline and never renews beyond it. TERM/alarm unwind the role's release; the supervisor additionally releases by exact owner after death and on restart. A process generation is recorded before work; recovery cannot kill a reused pid. If the forge is unreachable, the supervisor waits for lease expiry before calling the job finished. The old independent train lease and runner timeout settings are removed. 3. The control-plane lease derives directly from `BUDGET_S`: reporting takes no extra lease. Every request expires dead work, and a lease ending without a result records `died`, clears the runner slot, and never requeues a gate or train. A late result cannot overwrite that answer; another runner cannot finish the holder's job. 4. A runner gate job runs `scripts/gate-dev --pr N`, the real stages and per-test content-addressed proofs, under the same enforced budget. A missing whole-gate attestation is not a negative substitute for executing the gate. The real driver chooses its tooling before one preflight, overlaps independent stages, shares read-only Python syntax within a structure run and overlaps independent forge reads and result consumers. Exact input keys remain mandatory for reuse; the bypass and whole-gate attestation are never timing evidence for outcome (c). 5. `service.token_holders` names every credential consumer. A missing mandatory environment file stops systemd; refused claims, heartbeats, results and journal requests name the stale credential. Result reasons and phase times are relayed through the Worker's event log. The coordinator updates every inventory holder on rotation. 6. A base push queues one `deploy` job (GitHub and Origin), ahead of normal work. In that same enforced budget it compares `DEPLOYED_TREE`, uploads the merged Worker only when changed, and preserves secret bindings. A failed or dead deploy is retried with backoff; only one pending deploy survives newer base pushes. No between-poll deploy path remains. Job v2 replaces v1 atomically; every reference and generated type moves with it. 7. Closed/merged PRs cancel queued work and leave a persistent closed barrier against late `pr.ready` or push events; only an explicit reopen clears it. Supersession, TTL, lease expiry and runner retirement prevent stale work from rebuilding the backlog. Evidence: [audit](../units/TRAIN-DEFINITIVE/audit.md), [plan](../units/TRAIN-DEFINITIVE/plan.md), [rollout](../units/TRAIN-DEFINITIVE/rollout.md). The first Worker deploy and VM environment/unit update belong to its coordinator. A passing bypass is never reported as acceptance of the real-gate timing requirement. Owner acceptance amendment — Daniel, 2026-11-02 22:19 BRT: outcome (c) of TRAIN-DEFINITIVE is closed at the measured 10.523-second ordinary gate (`ea020515d` run) and approximately 16.8-second cold run. Stop further speed work in PR #1086; its opportunity cost is now the bottleneck. The enforced ten-second VM job budget is unchanged, and the ten-second performance target continues in a later unit. This accepts the current delivery without claiming the measurements are below ten seconds. Exact evidence remains in [validation](../units/TRAIN-DEFINITIVE/validation.md). | Premises: none | `scripts/tests/test_service_runner.py` | recorded |

It does not know, and keeps no field for, goals, objectives, requesters, projects, programs in the management sense, priority, hierarchy, which information matters (time, cost and success are data in the log that management chooses to read), metrics, dashboards, alerts, reports, billing, which executor to use and when, or who may create work. The solution (docs/programs/padrao-dev/solucao.md) is a model of the engineering life cycle kept in this repository: the metamodel as data in config/model/ (one file per object type, link type, system and inventory source, each checked against contracts/model/), one file per object in the authoritative source of each system, links that record the target's fingerprint and turn suspect when it changes, states derived from links, and conformance calculated per system on the N/P/L/F scale (ISO/IEC 33020); scripts/model checks, shows and measures it. The main metric is the share of active requirements whose chain (requirement, architecture element, unit, test, validation) is verified end to end; the guards are the requirement inventory (no source left out), the escape rate (10.9 % at the baseline), throughput, the gate's 60 s, AWS under US$ 200 a month and the 1 s median deploy. A dependency on the same or a lower layer is accepted and declared on the element with this decision as its basis (12 of the 29); one that goes up is a defect to break by moving the code or inverting the dependency (17, listed in docs/programs/padrao-dev/phases/F8.md). STD-architecture-layers refuses a declared dependency that goes up, so no upward dependency is ever accepted by declaring it. The reflexion share goes from 42 % to 65 % (34 convergences, 17 divergences, 1 absence); VAL-changeability asks 85 %, which the 17 breaks reach.
